❯ how would you improve this apps AI tone matching capability to get the        
  closest sound to an artist, album, or song, possible.                         

⏺ I'll look at how tone matching works today before suggesting anything.

  Searched for 1 pattern, read 1 file, ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Now the matcher implementation and the architecture notes on it.

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Let me check the cloud/LLM side: the audio telemetry fed to the model and how
  the gear research feeds the choice.

  Searched for 2 patterns, ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ I read through the pipeline: rig_local_tone_matcher.mm, local_tone_learning.h,
  the gear-research and telemetry code in rig_ai_support.mm, and the Learn &
  Match wiring in nam_rig_ui.mm:3690-3730. Here's what I'd change, ordered by
  how much closer to the target each change should get you for the work
  involved.

  Where it falls short today

  - Choosing the gear is the biggest lever, and very little gear gets tried.
    rigLocalToneSeeds tests at most 3 amps × 2 cabs, then tunes knobs only on
    the single best one. Knob tuning can't turn a Plexi into a 5150.
  - Gain is barely tuned. The search moves 6 controls: amp drive, bass, mid,
    treble and the two cab cuts. Input gain, pedal/overdrive drive, presence and
    power stage aren't searched, and those decide how saturated the tone is.
  - The comparison is coarse. It uses 11 wide, overlapping band energies plus
    two dynamics numbers, measured on a single 1.5-second excerpt. That captures
    EQ tilt but misses cab resonances (the 2–5 kHz peaks), the character of the
    distortion (fizz vs. smooth), and compression.
  - The search model is linear. It's trained on 13 renders that each nudge one
    knob by ±1 step from the starting point. Drive behaves very nonlinearly, so
    the model's predictions break down away from that starting point.
  - Mix and separation artifacts count against the match. The target stem still
    carries mix EQ, bus compression, reverb and Demucs artifacts. Nothing
    compensates for them, so the search bends amp and cab settings to imitate
    the mix.

  Improvements, in priority order

  1. Screen cabs without re-rendering the amp. A cab IR is linear. Render each
     amp once, then use FFT convolution to try every installed IR against that
     amp output, which takes milliseconds per IR. If power-stage processing sits
     after the amp model, include it in the per-amp render. This changes cab
     choice from "2 options" to "the whole library." It's the cheapest large
     improvement.
  2. Screen more amps, using the gear research. Run the gear-research step
     (artist → amps/cabs/pedals) before Learn & Match, not only from the cached
     results. Pre-render 20–40 candidate amps on a short excerpt, and tune knobs
     on the top 2–3 rather than one.
  3. Add a final EQ correction step. After the gear and knob search, fit the
     Post Pin EQ directly to the remaining spectral difference: smoothed,
     limited to about ±6 dB, with a few bands. It needs no renders. It absorbs
     the mix and mastering EQ so the amp and cab search doesn't have to fake it.
     Show it separately as "mix EQ" so users can turn it off.
  4. Compare more meaningful features:
     - 1/3-octave or ERB bands (~30) instead of 11 wide ones, with weighting by
       hearing sensitivity.
     - Saturation features that don't depend much on what was played: spectral
       flatness per band, an estimate of harmonic density, and how fast the
       level falls after each note starts (sag/compression).
     - Optionally, distance between learned audio embeddings (CLAP, OpenL3 or
       MERT run locally) as a second score. They capture timbre in ways
       hand-built features don't.
  5. Remove the effect of playing different parts. Today the DI and the target
     are different performances, which the code itself flags as a bias.
     - Add a play-along mode: loop the target section, record the DI while the
       user plays the same riff, and line the two up note-by-note with onset
       alignment (DTW). Comparing aligned frames is far stronger than comparing
       whole-excerpt averages.
     - Otherwise, average over many excerpts (for example, separately for palm
       mutes and for sustained chords) instead of the single loudest 1.5
       seconds.
  6. Use a better optimizer with a wider search. Replace the one-shot ridge
     model with a few rounds of re-fitting around the current best, or with
     CMA-ES or Bayesian optimization on real renders. Add input gain, pedal
     drive/tone, presence, depth and sag to the searched controls. Keep the
     existing rule that only real renders can become finalists.
  7. Make the Demucs artifacts symmetric. Either lower the weight of bands where
     separation is unreliable (above ~8 kHz, and the low end where bass bleeds
     in), or add a matching amount of degradation to the candidate renders
     before comparing.
  8. Reuse training data across runs. The per-run JSON files under Local Tone 
     Training/ are saved but never read back. Across many runs they could become
     a global model of how each capture responds to its knobs, which would make
     later matches start closer to the target.
  9. Combine with the cloud copilot. Use the LLM for the musical side (artist →
     gear and settings) and the local matcher for the measured side. Today
     they're separate buttons. A single flow would go gear research → wide
     screen → local tuning → EQ correction → A/B test.

  If you want to start somewhere, I'd do #1, #3 and adding input/pedal gain to
  #6 first. They're small, self-contained changes inside
  rig_local_tone_matcher.mm and local_tone_learning.h, and should give the
  biggest jump. I can sketch or implement any of these.

     Local tone matcher v2 — implementation plan (all 9 improvements)

     Context

     Learn & Match (src/rig_local_tone_matcher.mm, src/local_tone_learning.h)
     gets close only in broad EQ terms. The main limits:
     - It tries at most 3 amps × 2 cabs and tunes 6 knobs on a single linear
       model.
     - It scores on 11 wide bands from one 1.5-second excerpt.
     - It only ever sees the first 20 s of the Demucs stem (decode() in
       rig_riff_recorder.mm, kSeconds).
     - It has no correction for mix EQ or Demucs artifacts.

     Goal: a measurably closer match within ~2–4 minutes on A2 / True 8x / 96k,
     using the same guarantees as today:
     - Finalists come only from real renders.
     - The live rig stays untouched.
     - Nothing is uploaded.
     - Apply & Save stays explicit.

     User decisions:
     - AI gear research runs only behind an opt-in checkbox, default off, text 
       only. Cached research is always used.
     - Time budget is ~2–4 minutes.

     Facts from exploration that shape the design

     - The post-cab chain is linear and time-invariant (nam_rig_plugin.cpp
       processPostChain ~1508–1833) when Cab B, compression and space FX are
       off, which the seeds already ensure. The chain is: cab IR → cab low/high
       cut → Cab Console pins (131–154, enable 78) → DC blocker →
       Bass/Mid/Treble (12/13/14, post-cab shelves/peak at 150/700/3k) → output.
     - Because linear filters commute, two things follow:
       - (a) Render the amp once with the cab off, then convolve offline with
         any IR. This equals a real render with that cab, up to gain.
       - (b) Bass/mid/treble, cab cuts and Console pins can be solved in closed 
         form with no renders.
     - Seeds turn off power (74), transformer (76) and speaker (77). The amp,
       its pedal and the overdrive are the only nonlinear parts.
     - WavIR::load (src/wav_ir.h) resamples and truncates the IR to 0.17 s, but
       it doesn't expose the taps. The resampler is file-static at
       wav_ir.cpp:102.
     - DSP::RealFFT and zvma exist in src/dsp_accel.h.
     - Gear research (-[RigAICopilot 
       researchGearForPrompt:presetContext:config:localCLI:requestID:],
       rig_ai_copilot.mm:1669) is private and gated on self.requestID. Its JSON
       has amps/cabs/pedals/search_terms.
     - The recorder's startRecordingGuided: stops all playback, and playback
       uses NSSound. Nothing exists yet for playing along.

     Build order (each phase is shippable and tested)

     ┌───────┬───────────────────────────────────────┬─────────────────────────┐
     │ Phase │                Bullets                │        Why first        │
     ├───────┼───────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────┤
     │ A     │ #4 features v2, #5a target range      │ Every later step scores │
     │       │                                       │  with these             │
     ├───────┼───────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────┤
     │ B     │ #1 cab screen, #6 linear EQ solve +   │ Biggest accuracy gain   │
     │       │ nonlinear optimizer                   │                         │
     ├───────┼───────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────┤
     │ C     │ #3 mix EQ, #2 amp screen + gear       │ Builds on the B         │
     │       │ research                              │ machinery               │
     ├───────┼───────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────┤
     │ D     │ #7 Demucs-aware scoring, #8 training  │ Refinements             │
     │       │ reuse                                 │                         │
     ├───────┼───────────────────────────────────────┼─────────────────────────┤
     │ E     │ #5b play-along + alignment, #4b       │ Most UI work            │
     │       │ embeddings, #9 unified flow           │                         │
     └───────┴───────────────────────────────────────┴─────────────────────────┘

     ---

     #4 — Perceptual, performance-tolerant features (Phase A)

     File: src/local_tone_learning.h (stays header-only and dependency-free).
     - Bands: replace the 11 bands with 1/3-octave bands at 80 Hz–10 kHz (~22
       bands, Q≈4.3) in the existing biquad structure. Keep the active-window
       gating and the RMS-relative dB.
     - New saturation and dynamics features, added to Features:
       - Crest factor per octave band in 3 regions: low <400 Hz, mid 400–2.5k,
         high >2.5k. Distortion lowers crest.
       - Spectral flatness in the same 3 regions. Use a small in-header radix-2
         FFT over 2048-sample frames, active frames only.
       - Note decay slope: median dB/s over the 20 ms window energies after each
         onset. This measures sag and compression.
       - Keep the existing crest and dynamic-range features.
     - Weights: distance() takes a weight vector: an equal-loudness-ish tilt
       across bands and a per-group weight for the extras. kFeatures stays
       constexpr so Surrogate keeps working.
     - Multi-excerpt: in the .mm, excerpt() becomes excerpts(). It picks 3
       non-overlapping 1.5 s high-energy windows from DI and target. The DI
       windows are joined with 100 ms of silence into a single render. Features
       are measured per window and averaged.
     - Records: bump the training JSON to version: 2 and method: 
       local-surrogate-v2.
     - Tests (tests/verify_local_tone_learning.cpp):
       - The existing level, rate and stereo invariance checks still pass under
         the new tolerances.
       - Soft-clipping a sine lowers band crest and raises flatness.
       - A sine with a faster decay gives a more negative decay slope.
       - The FFT matches a naive DFT.

     #5a — Choose which part of the target to match (Phase A)

     Files: src/rig_riff_recorder.{h,mm}, src/nam_rig_ui.mm
     (importCopilotReference: ~3409, loadReferenceFromURL path).
     - Decode references with ExtAudioFile. This fixes the bug where mp3/m4a are
       offered in the picker but fail to load.
     - Keep up to 6 minutes of reference. Drop the 40 MB cap for references
       only; the DI cap stays at 20 s.
     - Add referenceSelectionStart and referenceSelectionLength properties (5–20
       s, default "Auto").
     - The existing "densest 20 s window" code (loadReferenceFromURL ~305–343)
       becomes the Auto choice and now actually runs.
     - referenceFrames returns only the selected section, so the matcher API
       doesn't change.
     - UI: the copilot drawer's target row gets Start (mm:ss stepper), Length
       and Auto, plus "Play selection" through the existing
       playTrack:@"reference".
     - Record referenceSource (demucs / user stem / full mix) for #7.
     - Test: extend tests/test_tone_workflow_ui.py to check that the range
       controls and selection properties exist. Add an Obj-C check that a 60 s
       synthetic WAV keeps its full length and Auto picks the loud section.

     #6 — Linear EQ solved in closed form + a real nonlinear optimizer (Phase B)

     New header: src/local_tone_post_eq.h. It reproduces the post-cab linear
     filters offline using the same coefficient formulas as nam_rig_plugin.cpp
     1739–1833:
     - the cab low cut (26) and high cut (27);
     - the 6 Console pins (shape/freq/gain/Q at 131+4i);
     - Bass/Mid/Treble.

     Three functions:
     - magnitudeDb(params, freq, rate) — analytic response at the band centers.
     - apply(params, audio) — the actual filtering, for verification renders.
     - solveLinear(rendered features, goal, bounds) — a small bounded
       least-squares fit of bass/mid/treble/cuts to the band residual. Gains
       enter in dB, which is nearly linear in band dB. Cuts are searched on a
       1-D grid.

     Optimizer (local_tone_learning.h plus the matcher):
     - Only the nonlinear controls need real renders:
       - amp_drive (22)
       - input_level (4), only if the base has no pedal
       - when OD is on: od_drive (179) and od_tone (180)
       - when the power stage is on: presence (34), depth (35) and sag (36)
     - Generalize Surrogate to a runtime dimension (std::vector). Add a
       quadratic ridge model.
     - Run trust-region rounds. Each round:
       - places a small central-composite design around the current best;
       - scores every render after solveLinear, so the score is the best
         achievable EQ for that gain setting;
       - re-fits, re-centers and shrinks the radius.
     - About 3 rounds × 6–9 renders, run on the top 2 (amp, cab) pairs.
     - Discrete options tested once on the best pair: OD on/off, and power stage
       on at default settings.
     - Keep the existing rules: finalists come only from real renders, headroom
       correction is verified, and cancellation is checked between renders.
     - Render cost: use the user's oversampling for the optimizer and finalists
       only.

     Tests (tests/verify_local_tone_matcher.mm):
     - With the stub renderer, the linear solve recovers a known bass/treble
       offset with no extra renders.
     - The quadratic model finds the drive optimum of a synthetic nonlinear stub
       (stub scales clipping by port 22).
     - The render count stays under budget.

     verify_local_match_engine (NAMRIG_REAL_RENDER) compares
     local_tone_post_eq.h::apply with an engine render. Features must agree
     within 0.1 dB.

     #1 — Screen every cab IR offline (Phase B)

     - In src/wav_ir.{h,cpp}, add static std::vector<float> 
       WavIR::loadTaps(const char* path, double rate, int channel = -1). It
       reuses the existing decode, resample and 0.17 s truncation code. Gain
       doesn't matter because features are RMS-relative.
     - In the matcher, add an FFT convolver (fftConvolve(x, ir) using
       DSP::RealFFT and DSP::zvma from dsp_accel.h). Link dsp_accel.cpp into the
       test build in tests/test_local_tone_matcher.py and the CMake
       verify_local_match_engine target.
     - Flow per amp:
       a. Render once with the cab off (port 9 = 0, stage 2 path cleared) and
          the cab cuts neutral.
       b. Convolve with every cab IR in the library: stage 2 entries from
          rigAILibraryCandidates(root, query, 400), deduped.
       c. Apply solveLinear, measure, rank, and keep the top 3 cabs.
     - Amps that already include a cab (rigAIModelIncludesCab) skip this step.
     - Cache taps per (path, rate) in memory for the run.
     - Real renders confirm the top pairs (the existing seed path).
     - rigLocalToneSeeds is replaced by a staged builder: amps go to the cab
       screen, which produces the pairs. The seeds.count <= 8 guard moves to the
       pair stage.
     - Test:
       - A stub renderer whose output plus IR is known. Check that screening
         ranks the IR used to build the target first.
       - Engine smoke test: the offline-convolution score equals the real
         cab-render score within 0.2 dB.

     #3 — Mix EQ correction (Phase C)

     - After the optimizer, fit the remaining residual using Console pins
       131–154:
       - Pin 0 is a low shelf, pin 5 a high shelf, pins 1–4 bells at the default
         frequencies, Q 0.9.
       - Solve the gains by ridge least squares on the band residual using
         magnitudeDb, with the residual clamped to ±6 dB.
       - Accept the result only if a real render lowers the score.
     - Add RigLocalToneMatch.mixEQ (BOOL) and the pin values.
     - UI (selectCopilotLocalMatch: ~3762): "Mix EQ (Console pins): on/off"
       toggle per finalist, plus a log line listing the gains. Turning it off
       restores the base pin values before A/B or Apply.
     - Test: a stub target tilted by a pin-shaped EQ is recovered within 0.5 dB
       per pin.

     #2 — Wider amp screen + opt-in gear research (Phase C)

     Research:
     - Add a public -[RigAICopilot researchGearForPrompt:(NSString*)p 
       completion:(void(^)(NSDictionary*, NSError*))c]. It wraps the private
       method with its own request ID, so it doesn't clash with the
       self.requestID gate.
     - Without a configured provider or CLI it fails quietly and the matcher
       continues with the cache only.
     - UI: a "Research gear with AI (text only)" checkbox in the drawer, default
       off and persisted in RigAIConfig. The ARCHITECTURE wording changes to
       "no provider request unless Research gear is checked; audio is never
       sent".

     Amp pool:
     - Up to 24 amps: rigAILibraryCandidates(root, query, 60) stage 1, which
       already boosts research gear tokens. Dedupe by rigAIProfileKey and prefer
       rigAIHighestCapacityVariant.
     - With "Search Tone3000 too" checked, fetch up to 3 matches (not 1) via
       tone3000FetchBestProfile for the top rigAISuggestGearQueries.

     Screening pass (keeps the 2–4 minute budget):
     - Use a 0.75 s excerpt and the reduced oversampling mode (ports 20/21;
       oversample_modes.h).
     - Convolve each amp-only render with the current top-3 cabs, from a quick
       cab screen on the first amp. Take the best score.
     - Send the top 3 amps to the full cab screen (#1) and the top 2 pairs to
       the optimizer (#6).
     - Research pedals mentioning a TS/overdrive set the OD-on discrete option.
       Matching pedal NAMs (stage 0) become one extra discrete option.

     Test:
     - A stub renderer ranks the "right" amp first among 10 fakes.
     - With the checkbox off there are no copilot calls; check with a counter on
       the injected research hook.

     #7 — Demucs-aware scoring (Phase D)

     - When referenceSource == demucs, weight the bands below 100 Hz (bass
       bleed) and above 8 kHz (separation smear) at 0.3×. These are weights in
       the distance() vector from #4.
     - Opt-in "Accurate re-score (slow)":
       - At import, keep the sum of the non-guitar Demucs stems for the selected
         section. Today the temp folder is deleted after guitar.wav is read
         (~3613–3642). Save the sum as a float buffer on the recorder.
       - For each of the 3 finalists: mix (finalist render + other stems) → run
         Demucs again → compare the extracted guitar with the target stem. This
         puts the same artifacts on both sides.
       - Run it off the main thread, reusing runDemucsOnFile:'s python
         discovery. Cancel works.
     - Test: check the weight vector against the source flag (unit). The
       re-score path is covered by a UI test that the control exists and is off
       by default.

     #8 — Reuse past training runs (Phase D)

     - v2 records store amp_key / cab_key (rigAIProfileKey), the
       nonlinear-parameter deltas and the feature deltas from the anchor.
     - New LocalToneMemory (in the .mm):
       - Loads records for the chosen amp key and builds a prior Jacobian.
       - Round 1 of the optimizer is ridge-regularized toward the prior instead
         of toward 0, and uses a smaller design (about half the renders) when
         there are 2 or more past runs.
       - Keep at most 500 records, oldest pruned.
     - Render cache:
       - Amp-only render features and audio go to ~/Library/Caches/Axe 
         FX/LocalMatch/, keyed by hash(DI excerpt, amp path, drive-related
         controls, oversample mode, rate).
       - LRU limit 500 MB.
       - Re-matching the same take against a different target skips the amp
         renders.
       - This is local derived audio, so ARCHITECTURE gets a note.
     - Test: a fake prior from synthetic records shrinks the render count while
       reaching the same optimum on the stub. Cache hits return identical
       features.

     #5b — Play-along recording + aligned scoring (Phase E)

     Recording:
     - Add an AVAudioPlayer reference player that clearRecording / stopPlayback
       doesn't stop.
     - A "Play along" button runs the 3 s countdown, then starts the selected
       target section and startRecordingGuided: in non-guided mode. The
       host-time offset is saved.

     Alignment (new src/local_tone_align.h, header-only):
     - Compute an onset envelope (spectral flux, 10 ms hop) for the DI render
       and the target.
     - Cross-correlate to get a coarse offset, then run banded DTW to map
       frames.
     - Add an alignedDistance that averages the per-frame band-dB difference
       over aligned voiced frames.

     Use: blend 0.5·stat + 0.5·aligned when the alignment confidence is above a
     threshold (normalized correlation peak > 0.5). Otherwise use only the
     statistics-based score, with a log message.

     Test: a synthetic riff time-shifted and jittered ±40 ms is recovered by
     DTW. A mismatched riff falls below the confidence threshold.

     #4b — Optional embedding re-rank (Phase E)

     - Add scripts/tone_embed.py (CLAP, installed on first use with pip install
       the way Demucs is). It takes WAV paths and returns cosine distances as
       JSON.
     - It runs only on the final 3 finalists, behind an "Embedding re-rank"
       checkbox, off by default.
     - Combined score = feature distance + λ·embedding distance. Both numbers
       appear in the finalist title.
     - Test: the Python unit test uses a mocked model, and the script is skipped
       if CLAP isn't installed.

     #9 — Unified "Match Tone" flow (Phase E)

     - Learn & Match becomes the pipeline: research (optional) → amp screen →
       cab screen → optimizer with the EQ solve → mix EQ → optional Demucs
       re-score or embedding re-rank → finalists.
     - The progress log shows each stage with its render count and score change.
     - Each finalist shows the gear rationale from research
       (rigAIGearResearchSummary), the measured score, and the mix-EQ toggle.
     - Optional "Ask AI to polish" (needs the research checkbox):
       - Sends the text residuals only (existing rigAIAcousticDeltaGuidance)
         plus the finalist's controls.
       - The returned proposal is rendered and kept only if the score doesn't
         get worse by more than 0.3 dB.
     - Preview Tone, when a DI and target are both present, offers "Run local
       match" instead of a guess based only on the cloud.

     ---

     Critical files

     - src/local_tone_learning.h — features, weights, FFT, generalized quadratic
       surrogate
     - src/local_tone_post_eq.h (new), src/local_tone_align.h (new)
     - src/rig_local_tone_matcher.{h,mm} — staged pipeline, screening,
       optimizer, mix EQ, memory and cache
     - src/wav_ir.{h,cpp} — loadTaps
     - src/rig_riff_recorder.{h,mm} — ExtAudioFile decode, long reference,
       selection, play-along player, other-stems buffer
     - src/rig_ai_copilot.{h,mm} — public researchGearForPrompt:completion:,
       RigAIConfig research flag
     - src/nam_rig_ui.mm (~3360–3850) — target range UI, checkboxes, finalist
       mix-EQ toggle, Demucs keep-other-stems
     - src/CMakeLists.txt (verify target sources),
       tests/test_local_tone_matcher.py, tests/verify_local_tone_*.{mm,cpp},
       ARCHITECTURE.md (local matching section)
     - No LV2 ports are added, so rig_control_contract.h doesn't change.

     Verification

     1. Per phase:
        - python3 tests/test_local_tone_matcher.py (learning and matcher stubs).
        - cmake --build build --target verify_local_match_engine -j8 && 
          build/src/verify_local_match_engine /tmp/lm-train <A2 amp.nam> for
          real-engine checks: post-EQ equivalence, convolution vs. real cab,
          render budget and timing.
     2. cmake --build build -j8, then ./tests/run_all.sh.
     3. Benchmark harness: a new tests/bench_local_match.mm under
        NAMRIG_REAL_RENDER, not in CI.
        - Build a target from a hidden "truth" preset (known amp, IR, drive and
          EQ), rendered from a real DI plus a random pin tilt as a stand-in for
          mix EQ.
        - Run v1 and v2. Report feature distance, whether the truth amp/cab was
          found, render count and wall time at A2 / True 8x / 96k.
        - Pass bar: v2 has a lower score than v1, finds the truth cab ranked
          first, and finishes in 4 minutes or less.
     4. Manual check: ./build.sh --no-menu --no-launch, then in Axe FX.app:
        a. Import a song (mp3).
        b. Pick a section.
        c. Record a DI.
        d. Run Learn & Match with research off, then on.
        e. Render the DI A/B.
        f. Toggle mix EQ.
        g. Apply & Save.
        h. Confirm the live rig is unchanged until Apply, and that no network
           calls happen with the checkboxes off.

⏺ The plan's approved, so I'm starting Phase A with the new matching features
  (#4). First I'll read the current tests so the changes stay compatible.

  numerical floor. I'll add a −40 dB floor relative to each region's peak bin.

⏺ The remaining swing comes from Hann window leakage, which depends on where
  each tone lands between bins. A 4-term Blackman-Harris window keeps sidelobes
  about 92 dB down, below the floor:

⏺ Phase A is done: richer matching features (#4) and choosing which part of the
  target to match (#5a). Everything builds with no warnings, and a real-engine
  run on your Orange OR50 A2 capture improved the match distance from 0.791 to
  0.146 dB in 73 s. I haven't opened the app to look at the drawer layout yet.

  #4 — Richer matching features (src/local_tone_learning.h,
  src/rig_local_tone_matcher.mm)
  - Finer EQ comparison: 22 1/3-octave bands from 80 Hz to 10 kHz, replacing the
    11 wide ones.
  - Saturation and dynamics:
    - how dense the distortion is (kurtosis) in low, mid and high regions;
    - how noisy vs. tonal each region is (spectral flatness);
    - how fast notes decay after the pick attack.
  - Sample rate doesn't affect scores: my first version did. Switching the
    analysis to a fixed 23.4 Hz frequency grid fixed it: a 48k vs 44.1k
    recording of the same signal now differs by 0.08, under the test's 0.15
    limit.
  - Three excerpts instead of one: each candidate is scored on up to three
    separate loud 1.5 s excerpts. The DI pieces are joined with gaps so each
    candidate still needs only one render.
  - Training records: bumped to version: 2, and they now record how many
    excerpts were used.

  #5a — Choose the target section (src/rig_riff_recorder.{h,mm},
  src/nam_rig_ui.mm)
  - Whole song kept: the target used to be cut to its first 20 s, so the matcher
    only ever heard the intro. It now keeps the whole song, up to about 128 MB
    of audio.
  - More formats: decoding goes through CoreAudio, which fixes mp3/m4a files
    being offered in the file picker and then failing to load.
  - New controls on the target row: Auto, Start (m:ss with a stepper) and Length
    (5–20 s). Editing Start switches Auto off, and changing the section clears
    old finalists.
  - Demucs stems are now labelled as such. #7 will use this to discount Demucs
    artifacts.

  Tests
  - New checks for the feature math, a 6-second three-excerpt match, and the
    target section (60 s song, Auto finds the loud part, manual and clamped
    selections, an AAC .m4a).
  - The recorder test wasn't run by run_all.sh before; it's now part of
    tests/test_local_tone_matcher.py.
  - ./tests/run_all.sh has two failures, and both also fail on a clean checkout
    without my changes:
    - tests/test_a2_fast_ring_copy.py looks for int mirror_needed = in the
      NeuralAudio submodule code.
    - Ten deltaGuidance checks in tests/test_ai_tone.py fail
      (rigAIAcousticDeltaGuidance returns nothing). I haven't touched that code.

⏺ The fit is off (predicted 0.82 vs. verified 0.59) because sampling the EQ only
  at each band's center frequency misses how energy is actually distributed
  within sparse-harmonic bands. I'll fix this by averaging the EQ response
  across each band's width and adding a verified refinement loop that re-solves
  using measured error, without needing a full engine render.

⏺ The verified loop improves error from 2.53 to 0.22 dB (vs. 0.58 for a single
  solve), and the treble mismatch (−3.5 vs. −6) is explained by low signal
  energy above 3 kHz. I'll update the test to assert match quality overall,
  while only checking knob recovery where the signal actually has energy.

  seed presets. First the header:

       81 +std::vector<float> convolve(const std::vector<float>& x, const
          + std::vector<float>& h) {                                     
       82 +  size_t block = 256;                                         
       83 +  while (block < h.size()) block <<= 1;                       
       84 +  const size_t n = 2 * block, bins = block;                   
       85 +  DSP::RealFFT fft(n);

          + im[0] holds the real Nyquist bin.                            
       90 +  // Scale is irrelevant to the level-invariant features; test

       93 +    const size_t m = std::min(block, x.size() - start);

      415 +        struct Dim { uint32_t port; double step; };           
      416 +        // Best evaluation of `amp` whose control set matches 
          +`like` outside `dims`.                                        
      417 +        auto comparable = [&](const Evaluation& e, size_t amp,
          + const Controls& like, const std::vector<Dim>& dims) {        
      418 +          if (e.amp != amp || e.score > 1e299 || e.controls.si
          +ze() != like.size()) return false;                            
      419 +          for (const auto& [port, value] : like) {            
      420 +            if (!e.controls.count(port)) return false;

      426 +        };                                                    
      427 +        // Trust-region rounds: probe ±r per dimension, fit a 
          +quadratic (in step                                            
      428 +        // units) to every comparable evaluation, render its p
          +redicted minimum,                                             
      429 +        // re-center on the best real evaluation, shrink r.   
      430 +        auto search = [&](size_t amp, Controls center, const s
          +td::vector<Dim>& dims, const std::vector<double>& radii) {    
      431 +          const size_t d = dims.size(), terms = 1 + 2 * d + (d
          + == 2 ? 1 : 0);                                               
      432 +          for (double r : radii) {                            
      433 +            std::vector<Controls> probes;                     
      434 +            for (const Dim& dim : dims) for (double sign : {-1
          +.0, 1.0}) {                                                   
      435 +              Controls c = center; c[dim.port] += sign * r * d
          +im.step; probes.push_back(c);                                 
      436 +            }                                                 
      437 +            if (d == 2) { Controls c = center; for (const Dim&
          + dim : dims) c[dim.port] += .7 * r * dim.step; probes.push_bac
          +k(c); }                                                       
      438 +            for (const Controls& c : probes) { if (!budgetLeft
          +()) return; evaluate(amp, c); }                               
      439 +            auto basis = [&](const Controls& c) {             
      440 +              std::vector<double> phi{1};                     
      441 +              for (const Dim& dim : dims) { const double x = (
          +c.at(dim.port) - center.at(dim.port)) / dim.step; phi.push_bac
          +k(x); phi.push_back(x * x); }                                 
      442 +              if (d == 2) phi.push_back((c.at(dims[0].port) - 
          +center.at(dims[0].port)) / dims[0].step *                     
      443 +                                        (c.at(dims[1].port) - 
          +center.at(dims[1].port)) / dims[1].step);                     
      444 +              return phi;                                     
      445 +            };                                                
      446 +            std::vector<std::vector<double>> system(terms, std
          +::vector<double>(terms + 1, 0));                              
      447 +            size_t points = 0;                                
      448 +            for (const auto& e : evaluations) {               
      449 +              if (!comparable(e, amp, center, dims)) continue;
      450 +              const auto phi = basis(e.controls); ++points;   
      451 +              for (size_t i = 0; i < terms; ++i) {            
      452 +                for (size_t j = 0; j < terms; ++j) system[i][j
          +] += phi[i] * phi[j];                                         
      453 +                system[i][terms] += phi[i] * e.score;         
      454 +              }                                               
      455 +            }                                                 
      456 +            bool solved = points >= terms;                    
      457 +            for (size_t i = 1; i < terms && solved; ++i) syste
          +m[i][i] += 1e-3;                                              
      458 +            for (size_t col = 0; col < terms && solved; ++col)
          + {                                                            
      459 +              size_t pivot = col;                             
      460 +              for (size_t row = col + 1; row < terms; ++row) i
          +f (std::fabs(system[row][col]) > std::fabs(system[pivot][col])
          +) pivot = row;                                                
      461 +              std::swap(system[col], system[pivot]);          
      462 +              if (std::fabs(system[col][col]) < 1e-12) { solve
          +d = false; break; }                                           
      463 +              const double div = system[col][col];            
      464 +              for (double& v : system[col]) v /= div;         
      465 +              for (size_t row = 0; row < terms; ++row) if (row
          + != col) {                                                    
      466 +                const double factor = system[row][col];       
      467 +                for (size_t j = 0; j <= terms; ++j) system[row
          +][j] -= factor * system[col][j];                              
      468 +              }                                               
      469 +            }                                                 
      470 +            if (solved && budgetLeft()) {                     
      471 +              Controls bestPoint = center; double bestValue =

      474 +                Controls c = center;

       32 +// cannot imitate, written as a mono float32 WAV.             
       33 +NSString* writeCab(NSString* dir, NSString* name, std::initial
          +izer_list<double> peaks, double rate) {                       
       34 +  std::vector<float> h(1024, 0);                              
       35 +  h[0] = .3f;

       37 +    for (size_t n = 0; n < h.size(); ++n) h[n] += float(.25 * 
          +std::exp(-double(n) / 180) * std::sin(2 * M_PI * f * n / rate)
          +);

      76 +amp model is linear and time-invariant (`processPostChain`): ca
         +b IR → cab low/high cut                                        
      77 +→ Console pins (both only when a cab was processed and the cons
         +ole rack is on) → DC                                           
      78 +blocker → Bass (150 Hz low shelf) / Mid (700 Hz bell) / Treble 
         +(3 kHz high shelf),                                            
      79 +all RBJ biquads at Q 0.707. Linear filters commute, so an amp r
         +endered once with the                                          
      80 +cab off can be convolved with any IR (`WavIR::loadTaps`: the en
         +gine's exact resampled,                                        
      81 +170 ms-tapered taps; FFT overlap-add via `DSP::RealFFT`) and re
         +-voiced with

         + `pin_eq.h`

⏺ Phase B is done: cab screening (#1), and the EQ solver plus new optimizer
  (#6). Everything builds; the matcher tests and the full suite pass, apart from
  the same two failures that predate this work. I haven't opened the app to try
  Learn & Match from the drawer. Nothing is committed.

  How the matcher works now
  - Each amp is rendered once with the cab off. Everything after the amp (cab
    IR, cab cuts, Console pins, Bass/Mid/Treble) is linear, so that one render
    can be convolved offline with every cab IR in your library. Each pairing
    gets its own EQ fit, so a cab doesn't lose just because it needs different
    EQ.
  - The post-cab EQ is reproduced offline by a new header,
    src/local_tone_post_eq.h, using the engine's own filter formulas from
    pin_eq.h. Bass/Mid/Treble and the low cut are fitted directly against the
    measured audio, with no engine renders.
  - Only nonlinear settings use real renders: amp drive is searched in rounds of
    shrinking range on the best amp, with one round on the runner-up. The
    leading amp also tries an overdrive push and the power stage. The search is
    capped at 20 renders.
  - Finalists are still verified by full engine renders, and those real scores
    decide the ranking.

  Real-engine check
  I hid a known setting (cab B, +6 dB drive, bass +3, treble −4, 80 Hz low cut)
  behind your OR50 A2 capture, mixed among all 96 IRs in your library:
  - It found the right cab and recovered +6.0 dB drive, bass +3.0, treble −3.5
    and a 77 Hz low cut. The match went from 4.93 to 0.025 dB.
  - Offline and engine scores agreed to three decimals, including for real
    library IRs. This confirms the offline shortcut is exact.
  - It took 15 renders and 26 s with a 1.2 s test riff; screening all 99 IRs
    added only about 2 s.

  I haven't timed a realistic run (4 amps, three 1.5 s excerpts at 96k); my
  estimate is about 1.5–2.5 minutes.

  Fixes made along the way
  - EQ fit accuracy: my first EQ fit only got to 0.22 dB on a known target. It
    ignored that EQ also changes the texture features, and a coarse low-cut grid
    made the mid knob compensate. A second refinement pass over all features,
    with the low cut included, gets it to 0.017 dB and recovers the knob values.
  - FFT speed: the FFT I wrote in Phase A was recomputing sine and cosine for
    every butterfly. Measuring one candidate now takes 58 ms instead of 96 ms.

  Other changes
  - API: rigLocalToneSeeds is replaced by rigLocalTonePlan, which builds the
    list of amps (up to 4) and every WAV cab under your library's Cab folder (up
    to 400). The matcher now takes a plan: parameter.
  - Engine IR code: WavIR::loadTaps exposes the engine's exact IR taps, and
    WavIR::load now calls it. The plugin's audio behavior is unchanged.
  - Build: the LV2 UI module now also compiles dsp_accel.cpp and wav_ir.cpp and
    links Accelerate.
  - Run records: they're now version 3 and list every evaluation and finalist.
    Presets saved from a finalist keep only a summary.
  - Engine test harness: verify_local_match_engine takes an optional cab folder.

  Limitations
  - Cabs captured as .nam files aren't screened (you have none).
  - The old feature-space ridge surrogate in local_tone_learning.h is no longer
    used by the matcher, though it's still tested. I left it in case #8 (reusing
    past runs) wants it; I can delete it instead.

  Phase C is next: mix-EQ correction with the Console pins (#3), and the wider
  amp screen with opt-in gear research (#2).

⏺ The sequential fitting approach is flawed—the tone stack gets fitted first and
  distorts to mimic the mix EQ, leaving the pins too small to correct the error
  afterward. I'll fix this by refining pins and tone stack jointly, expanding
  the Gauss-Newton helper to 10 parameters with a ridge penalty applied only to
  the pins.

⏺ The real-engine run correctly identifies the amp, cab, and drive level with
  matching scores, but lands on an implausible EQ because the tone stack and pin
  gains overlap in frequency, creating a flat valley that Gauss-Newton wanders
  through. I'll add a small ridge penalty on the tone stack and a slightly
  stronger one on the pins to favor minimal adjustments while still allowing
  pins to correct real gear mismatches.

⏺ Found the issue: the joint fit starts from the extreme tone-only solution and
  Gauss-Newton can't walk it back far enough. I'll fix this by doing a one-shot
  joint solve with a ridge penalty on tone-stack values to land near the
  minimal-adjustment answer, then let Gauss-Newton refine from there.
